# Veracode Security Inspector

Portable contract for the shipped Veracode scan coverage, policy compliance, findings hygiene, SCA posture, and access control assessments over the Veracode Applications, Policy, Identity, Dynamic Analysis, Findings, Summary Report, and SCA Agent REST APIs.

## Purpose

Veracode Security Inspector gives auditors a read-only view of a Veracode Application Security Platform account: whether every application is scanned recently, successfully, and as often as its policies and business criticality require, whether Dynamic Analysis scans are authenticated and crawl their targets, whether applications pass their assigned policies, whether open flaws are fixed within their age limits with mitigations reviewed and false positive rates and flaw density under their ceilings, whether third-party libraries carry high-severity vulnerabilities or high-risk licenses and every application is covered by SCA, and who can see and administer the portfolio.

## Rationale

Veracode answers a refusal, an unlicensed product, and an empty account in ways a naive reader could mistake for a clean result. Dynamic Analysis and agent-based SCA are licensed separately, and a 401, 403, or 404 from either API usually means the product is unlicensed or the API user lacks its role. A portable implementation must keep those cases visible, so a refused or failed read never counts as an empty list, and an empty application, policy, workspace, analysis, user, role, or finding inventory is reported as unverifiable rather than compliant, except where the known runtime gaps say otherwise.

Most evidence comes from HAL lists walked page by page and from per-application, per-workspace, and per-analysis reads over a bounded sample. A walk that stops at its page cap, repeats a page, or keeps fewer records than the reported total_elements is partial, and a sample smaller than its population leaves part of the account unread. Either condition caps the checks built on that evidence, so a pass is reported only when everything it depends on was read in full.

## Runtime behavior

- The tool only reads. Every request is a GET signed with VERACODE-HMAC-SHA-256 from the API key ID and secret.
- A throttled or failed request is retried before it counts as failed: HTTP 429 and 5xx answers, timeouts, and network errors get up to 3 more attempts, each waiting as long as Retry-After asks up to 30 seconds, or 500, 1000, then 2000 milliseconds without one. A request that still fails names its path, the status, timeout, or network error, and the number of attempts, and the dataset it was reading is reported as not collected, never as an empty or partial list.
- Sampled reads cover the first max_applications applications (100 by default) for sandboxes, findings, summary reports, and linked SCA projects, the first max_workspaces SCA workspaces (25 by default), the first max_analyses Dynamic Analysis analyses (25 by default) with at most 10 scans each, and at most 200 active API accounts for credential records.
- Finding evidence lists keep their first 50 entries, except the overdue flaws, unreviewed and unjustified mitigations, high-severity SCA issues, high-risk license issues, users with access to every application, inactive users, users without a last login, and credentials over the maximum age, which keep 100. VERACODE-18 shows at most 20 linked projects per application. Every count and verdict comes from the full read, not from these previews.
- The policy compliance assessment makes no per-application requests, so its max_applications argument has no effect.
- Flaw age limits are fixed by severity at 30 days for Very High, 60 for High, 90 for Medium, and 180 for Low, and cannot be configured.
- The organization-wide team list is read with all_for_org=true. When Veracode refuses that, only the teams the API user belongs to are listed, and that view is treated as partial.
- Prescan module selection, Pipeline Scan and IDE scan use, and Collections compliance are not exposed by the REST APIs the tool reads, so VERACODE-11, VERACODE-14, and VERACODE-20 always return manual and name the evidence a reviewer must collect from the Veracode Platform.
- Dynamic Analysis scan configurations are kept only as a summary: authentication method names, crawl settings, the allowed host count, and a scrubbed target URL.

## Non-goals

- Changing Veracode settings, starting scans, approving mitigations, or issuing any administrative write request
- Reading the XML APIs, Pipeline Scan or IDE scan results, or the Collections API
- Judging the accuracy of individual findings or the substance of mitigation justifications beyond whether one is present
- Treating a refused read, an unlicensed product, or an empty inventory as a compliant result
- Reproducing a particular programming language, package layout, or command-line framework

## Portable implementation guidance

Keep the request signer, API client, pagination, sampling, evidence projection, verdict evaluation, and bundle writer as separable concerns. Count from the full collected arrays at the decision point rather than from evidence previews. Keep each dataset's read state (read, refused, not requested because the inventory it depends on was refused, or partial) next to its records, along with how many applications, workspaces, analyses, and API accounts were sampled out of how many, so every finding can say which sources it depended on and why a pass was capped.

## Shared integration contract

This specification requires [shared integration contract version 1.1](./integration-contract.md). The raw contract is available at https://raw.githubusercontent.com/ethanolivertroy/grclanker/main/specs/integration-contract.md.

## Known runtime gaps

- A list entry that is not a JSON object is dropped without marking the list partial, and before any page reports total_pages a full page holding one counts as short and ends the walk with the list complete.
- An empty 2xx list page, or one whose JSON is not an object, is read as a page holding no records, which ends the walk with the list complete when no total_pages has been reported.
- VERACODE-15 can pass when the application inventory is read and empty, though the other application checks treat an empty inventory as unverifiable.
- When the application inventory is unreadable or empty, the findings hygiene assessment reports VERACODE-16 and VERACODE-17 at high severity, copied from VERACODE-03's manual finding, instead of their own medium severity.
- VERACODE-16 skips a finding list that kept no finding before checking whether that list is partial, so a truncated empty list does not lower fp_rate_coverage_complete and the check can pass without that application's rate; changing that behavior requires a separate runtime fix.
- VERACODE-06 can pass when the license issue lists read hold no issue, even when the sampled workspaces hold no scanned library, though VERACODE-05 treats an empty library set as unverifiable.
- VERACODE-18 counts an application as covered when its profile enables upload-and-scan SCA or it has a linked SCA agent project, without checking that either has produced a scan.
- VERACODE-07 fails when the organization-wide team list kept no team even when that list is partial, for example when its only page is empty but reports a positive total_elements.
- The SCA REST API documentation asks for a user-agent header and accept-encoding: gzip on every request; the collector sets neither itself and relies on Node.js fetch, which sends user-agent: node and accept-encoding: gzip, deflate.

## Tools

| Tool | Purpose | Finding IDs | Result shape |
|---|---|---|---|
| `veracode_check_access` | Validate HMAC-signed read-only Veracode access across applications, policies, identity (users, teams, roles, API credentials), SCA Agent, and Dynamic Analysis surfaces, and report which roles are missing. | None | A text result whose structured details preserve the runtime assessment or access-check object byte-for-byte. |
| `veracode_assess_scan_coverage` | Assess Veracode scan coverage (controls 1, 4, 10, 11, 13, 14, 19): recent scan coverage, policy scan frequency, sandbox usage, prescan module coverage (manual), Dynamic Analysis configuration, pipeline integration (manual), and scan completion. | `VERACODE-01`, `VERACODE-04`, `VERACODE-10`, `VERACODE-11`, `VERACODE-13`, `VERACODE-14`, `VERACODE-19` | A text result whose structured details preserve the runtime assessment or access-check object byte-for-byte. |
| `veracode_assess_policy_compliance` | Assess Veracode policy compliance (controls 2, 15, 20): application policy_compliance_status, custom policy adoption with finding rules and grace periods, and collections posture (manual, with business-unit evidence). | `VERACODE-02`, `VERACODE-15`, `VERACODE-20` | A text result whose structured details preserve the runtime assessment or access-check object byte-for-byte. |
| `veracode_assess_findings_hygiene` | Assess Veracode findings hygiene (controls 3, 12, 16, 17): open flaw aging against severity SLAs, mitigation approval workflow, false positive rate from FP mitigation annotations, and Very High/High flaw density per KLOC from summary reports. | `VERACODE-03`, `VERACODE-12`, `VERACODE-16`, `VERACODE-17` | A text result whose structured details preserve the runtime assessment or access-check object byte-for-byte. |
| `veracode_assess_sca_posture` | Assess Veracode Software Composition Analysis posture (controls 5, 6, 18): open high-CVSS library vulnerabilities, HIGH risk license issues, and application SCA coverage through upload-and-scan SCA or linked agent projects. | `VERACODE-05`, `VERACODE-06`, `VERACODE-18` | A text result whose structured details preserve the runtime assessment or access-check object byte-for-byte. |
| `veracode_assess_access_controls` | Assess Veracode identity hygiene (controls 7, 8, 9): team-scoped least privilege, Administrator and inactive user audit with SAML evidence, and API credential age and expiration. | `VERACODE-07`, `VERACODE-08`, `VERACODE-09` | A text result whose structured details preserve the runtime assessment or access-check object byte-for-byte. |
| `veracode_export_audit_bundle` | Export a Veracode audit bundle with raw API snapshots (core_data/), normalized findings (analysis/), framework reports (compliance/), QUICK_REFERENCE.md, _errors.log on partial failure, and a zip archive. | `VERACODE-01`, `VERACODE-04`, `VERACODE-10`, `VERACODE-11`, `VERACODE-13`, `VERACODE-14`, `VERACODE-19`, `VERACODE-02`, `VERACODE-15`, `VERACODE-20`, `VERACODE-03`, `VERACODE-12`, `VERACODE-16`, `VERACODE-17`, `VERACODE-05`, `VERACODE-06`, `VERACODE-18`, `VERACODE-07`, `VERACODE-08`, `VERACODE-09` | A text result plus output directory, paired archive path, file count, finding count, and collection-error count. |

|---|---|---|---|

| `api_key_secret` | string | no | Veracode API key secret (hex). Defaults to VERACODE_API_KEY_SECRET, then the credentials file profile. |
| `region` | string | no | Veracode region: us (api.veracode.com), eu (api.veracode.eu), or us-fed (api.veracode.us). Defaults to VERACODE_REGION or us. |
| `base_url` | string | no | Explicit REST API base URL override. Defaults to the region host. |
| `profile` | string | no | Profile name inside ~/.veracode/credentials. Defaults to VERACODE_API_PROFILE or default. |
| `credentials_file` | string | no | Path to the INI credentials file. Defaults to ~/.veracode/credentials. |
| `timeout_seconds` | number | no | HTTP timeout in seconds. Defaults to 30. |

## Authentication

Supported modes:

- HMAC-signed API key pair: every request carries Authorization: VERACODE-HMAC-SHA-256 id={api_key_id},ts={timestamp},nonce={nonce},sig={signature}, where timestamp is the current time in epoch milliseconds, nonce is 16 random bytes as 32 lowercase hex digits, and signature is the lowercase hex HMAC-SHA256 of id={api_key_id}&host={host}&url={path and query}&method={METHOD} under a signing key derived from the hex-decoded api_key_secret by three chained HMAC-SHA256 steps over the nonce bytes, the timestamp digits, and vcode_request_version_1

Credential precedence, highest first:

1. Explicit tool arguments
2. VERACODE_* environment variables
3. The selected profile of the Veracode credentials file, read only for whichever of the key ID and secret is still missing
4. A base_url from any source takes precedence over the region; with no region, us is used

Environment variables: `VERACODE_API_PROFILE`, `VERACODE_API_CREDENTIALS_FILE`, `VERACODE_API_KEY_ID`, `VERACODE_API_KEY_SECRET`, `VERACODE_REGION`, `VERACODE_API_BASE_URL`, `VERACODE_TIMEOUT`

Configuration locations: ~/.veracode/credentials, Explicit path from credentials_file or VERACODE_API_CREDENTIALS_FILE

Credential and deployment variants: Regions us and commercial (https://api.veracode.com, the default), eu, europe, and european (https://api.veracode.eu), and fedramp, us-fed, and federal (https://api.veracode.us), matched after trimming and lowercasing; any other region is rejected unless a base_url is given, An explicit base_url replaces the region's host, with any query, fragment, and trailing slashes removed, Named INI profiles in the credentials file, selected by profile or VERACODE_API_PROFILE, else the default profile; the api_key_secret must be a hex string

Configuration fields: `veracode_api_key_id`, `veracode_api_key_secret`

Malformed configuration: Reject malformed or ambiguous configuration before any request; never echo credential values.

## Permissions

| Kind | Permission, role, or plan | Unlocks | Notes |
|---|---|---|---|
| role | `Any Veracode API user` | `self`, `self_api_credentials` | The Identity REST API documentation lets an account without the Admin API or Administrator role read its own user record and API credentials. |
| role | `Security Insights or Reviewer API role` | `applications`, `policies`, `sandboxes` | veracode_check_access names this role for applications and policies; the Applications REST API documentation names a user account with the Creator or Security Lead role. |
| license | `Dynamic Analysis license and the Security Insights API role` | `dynamic_analyses`, `dynamic_analysis_scans`, `dynamic_scan_configurations` | veracode_check_access names this role; the Dynamic Analysis REST API documentation names an API user account with the Upload and Scan API role. |
| role | `Results API role` | `findings`, `summary_reports` | veracode_check_access does not probe these APIs; the Findings and Summary Report REST API documentation names an API user account with the Results API role, or a user account with the Reviewer or Security Lead role. |
| license | `SCA license and the Workspace Administrator or Workspace Editor role` | `sca_workspaces`, `sca_vulnerability_issues`, `sca_license_issues`, `sca_libraries`, `sca_application_projects` | veracode_check_access names these roles for the workspace list; the SCA REST API documentation requires the API credentials of a UI user account and does not support API user accounts. |
| role | `Administrator role` | `users`, `teams`, `roles`, `api_credentials` | veracode_check_access names this role for users, teams, and roles; the Identity REST API documentation names an API user account with the Admin API role or a user account with the Administrator role, and reading another user's API credentials needs the Administrator role or being that user's administrator. |

## API surfaces

| ID | Interface | Read operation | Service or client | IAM action | Intent | Projection stage | Fields consumed | Reference |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| `self` | HTTP | `GET /api/authn/v2/users/self` | Veracode Identity REST API | N/A | read | The access control assessment exports the record as read through the export's credential pass; the other assessments request it without exporting it, and veracode_check_access reads it for the principal and its roles. | `user_id`, `user_name`, `email_address`, `roles.role_name` | [Official documentation](https://docs.veracode.com/r/c_identity_intro) |
| `applications` | HTTP | `GET /appsec/v1/applications` | Veracode Applications REST API | N/A | read | The collector exports the walk's result as read, every returned record with all its fields alongside pagesFetched, complete, and, when known, totalPages, totalElements, and notes, through the export's credential pass; decision facts read the listed fields. | `guid`, `profile.name`, `profile.policies.guid`, `profile.policies.policy_compliance_status`, `profile.business_criticality`, `profile.business_unit.name`, `profile.teams`, `profile.upload_and_scan_sca_enabled`, `scans.scan_type`, `scans.status`, `scans.modified_date`, `last_completed_scan_date` | [Official documentation](https://docs.veracode.com/r/c_apps_intro) |
| `policies` | HTTP | `GET /appsec/v1/policies` | Veracode Policy REST API | N/A | read | The collector exports the walk's result as read, every returned record with all its fields alongside pagesFetched, complete, and, when known, totalPages, totalElements, and notes, through the export's credential pass; decision facts read the listed fields. | `guid`, `name`, `type`, `finding_rules`, `sev0_grace_period`, `sev1_grace_period`, `sev2_grace_period`, `sev3_grace_period`, `sev4_grace_period`, `sev5_grace_period`, `scan_frequency_rules.scan_type`, `scan_frequency_rules.frequency` | [Official documentation](https://api.swaggerhub.com/apis/Veracode/veracode-policy_api_specification/1.0) |
| `sandboxes` | HTTP | `GET /appsec/v1/applications/{application_guid}/sandboxes` | Veracode Applications REST API | N/A | read | The collector exports each sampled application's sandbox records as read, keyed by the application's guid read as text (empty text when it has none), without the walk's pagination state, through the export's credential pass; decisions count the records and read no field. | None | [Official documentation](https://docs.veracode.com/r/c_apps_intro) |
| `dynamic_analyses` | HTTP | `GET /was/configservice/v1/analyses` | Veracode Dynamic Analysis REST API | N/A | read | The collector exports the returned analysis records as read, without the walk's pagination state, through the export's credential pass; decisions count the records, and each sampled analysis's analysis_id selects its scan list. | `analysis_id`, `name` | [Official documentation](https://docs.veracode.com/r/c_dynamic_intro) |
| `dynamic_analysis_scans` | HTTP | `GET /was/configservice/v1/analyses/{analysis_id}/scans` | Veracode Dynamic Analysis REST API | N/A | read | The collector keeps each returned scan's scan_id read as text, else null, and its target_url read as text with any userinfo and query replaced by [REDACTED] and any fragment dropped, else null; every other field is dropped, and the scans are exported keyed by the analysis_id through the export's credential pass. Decisions read scan_id to select the configurations to inspect. | `scan_id`, `target_url` | [Official documentation](https://docs.veracode.com/r/c_dynamic_intro) |
| `dynamic_scan_configurations` | HTTP | `GET /was/configservice/v1/scans/{scan_id}/configuration` | Veracode Dynamic Analysis REST API | N/A | read | The collector keeps, per inspected scan, its analysis_id and scan_id; target_url (its url when it is an object) read as text with any userinfo and query replaced by [REDACTED] and any fragment dropped, else null; the member names of auth_configuration.authentications, with one [REDACTED] marker in place of their contents; crawl_configuration.disabled read as a boolean, else null; whether crawl_configuration.crawl_script_data is a JSON object; and the number of JSON object entries in scan_setting.allowed_hosts, else allowed_hosts. Every other field is dropped, and the result is exported through the export's credential pass. | `auth_configuration.authentications`, `crawl_configuration.disabled`, `crawl_configuration.crawl_script_data`, `target_url`, `target_url.url`, `scan_setting.allowed_hosts`, `allowed_hosts` | [Official documentation](https://docs.veracode.com/r/c_dynamic_intro) |
| `findings` | HTTP | `GET /appsec/v2/applications/{application_guid}/findings` | Veracode Findings REST API | N/A | read | The collector exports each sampled application's finding list as the walk returned it, every returned record with all its fields alongside pagesFetched, complete, and, when known, totalPages, totalElements, and notes, keyed by the application's guid read as text (empty text when it has none), through the export's credential pass; decision facts read the listed fields. | `issue_id`, `finding_details.severity`, `finding_status.status`, `finding_status.resolution`, `finding_status.resolution_status`, `finding_status.first_found_date`, `annotations.action`, `annotations.comment` | [Official documentation](https://docs.veracode.com/r/c_findings_v2_intro) |
| `summary_reports` | HTTP | `GET /appsec/v2/applications/{application_guid}/summary_report` | Veracode Summary Report REST API | N/A | read | The collector exports each sampled application's summary report as read, keyed by the application's guid read as text (empty text when it has none), through the export's credential pass; decision facts read the listed fields. | `static_analysis.modules.module.loc`, `static_analysis.modules.module.numflawssev5`, `static_analysis.modules.module.numflawssev4` | [Official documentation](https://docs.veracode.com/r/c_rest_summary_report_intro) |
| `sca_workspaces` | HTTP | `GET /srcclr/v3/workspaces` | Veracode SCA Agent REST API | N/A | read | The collector exports the walk's result as read, every returned record with all its fields alongside pagesFetched, complete, and, when known, totalPages, totalElements, and notes, through the export's credential pass; decision facts read the listed fields. | `id`, `name` | [Official documentation](https://docs.veracode.com/r/c_sourceclear_intro) |
| `sca_vulnerability_issues` | HTTP | `GET /srcclr/v3/workspaces/{workspace_id}/issues?type=vulnerability&status=open` | Veracode SCA Agent REST API | N/A | read | The collector exports each sampled workspace's vulnerability issue list as the walk returned it, every returned record with all its fields alongside pagesFetched, complete, and, when known, totalPages, totalElements, and notes, as the vulnerabilities member of an entry keyed by the workspace's id read as text (empty text when it has none), through the export's credential pass; decision facts read the listed fields. | `severity`, `vulnerability.cvss3_score`, `vulnerability.cvss2_score`, `vulnerability.cve`, `library.name` | [Official documentation](https://docs.veracode.com/r/c_sourceclear_intro) |
| `sca_license_issues` | HTTP | `GET /srcclr/v3/workspaces/{workspace_id}/issues?type=license&status=open` | Veracode SCA Agent REST API | N/A | read | The collector exports each sampled workspace's license issue list as the walk returned it, every returned record with all its fields alongside pagesFetched, complete, and, when known, totalPages, totalElements, and notes, as the licenses member of an entry keyed by the workspace's id read as text (empty text when it has none), through the export's credential pass; decision facts read the listed fields. | `license.risk`, `license.name`, `library.name` | [Official documentation](https://docs.veracode.com/r/c_sourceclear_intro) |
| `sca_libraries` | HTTP | `GET /srcclr/v3/workspaces/{workspace_id}/libraries` | Veracode SCA Agent REST API | N/A | read | The collector exports each sampled workspace's library list as the walk returned it, every returned record with all its fields alongside pagesFetched, complete, and, when known, totalPages, totalElements, and notes, as the libraries member of an entry keyed by the workspace's id read as text (empty text when it has none), through the export's credential pass; decisions count the records and read no field. | None | [Official documentation](https://docs.veracode.com/r/c_sourceclear_intro) |
| `sca_application_projects` | HTTP | `GET /srcclr/v3/applications/{application_guid}/projects` | Veracode SCA Agent REST API | N/A | read | The collector exports each requested linked project response as read, keyed by the application's guid read as text (empty text when it has none), through the export's credential pass; decision facts read the listed fields. When no list was requested the dataset is a marker naming the reason, which calls the SCA Agent API not readable both when its workspace list was refused and when it held no workspace. | `linked_projects.name`, `linked_projects.id`, `linked_projects.workspace.name`, `linked_projects.last_scan_date` | [Official documentation](https://docs.veracode.com/r/c_sourceclear_intro) |
| `users` | HTTP | `GET /api/authn/v2/users` | Veracode Identity REST API | N/A | read | The collector exports the walk's result as read, every returned record with all its fields alongside pagesFetched, complete, and, when known, totalPages, totalElements, and notes, through the export's credential pass; decision facts read the listed fields. | `user_id`, `user_name`, `email_address`, `active`, `login_enabled`, `account_type`, `api_user`, `roles.role_name`, `teams`, `no_teams_required`, `last_login`, `saml_user` | [Official documentation](https://docs.veracode.com/r/c_identity_intro) |
| `teams` | HTTP | `GET /api/authn/v2/teams` | Veracode Identity REST API | N/A | read | The collector exports the walk's result as read, every returned record with all its fields alongside pagesFetched, complete, and, when known, totalPages, totalElements, and notes, through the export's credential pass; decisions count the records and read no field. After a restart without all_for_org, notes ends with "all_for_org=true was refused (<status>), so only teams the API user is a member of were listed and the team inventory is a partial view." | None | [Official documentation](https://docs.veracode.com/r/c_identity_intro) |
| `roles` | HTTP | `GET /api/authn/v2/roles` | Veracode Identity REST API | N/A | read | The collector exports the walk's result as read, every returned record with all its fields alongside pagesFetched, complete, and, when known, totalPages, totalElements, and notes, through the export's credential pass; decision facts read the listed fields. | `role_name`, `ignore_team_restrictions` | [Official documentation](https://docs.veracode.com/r/c_identity_intro) |
| `api_credentials` | HTTP | `GET /api/authn/v2/api_credentials/user_id/{user_id}` | Veracode Identity REST API | N/A | read | The collector keeps each sampled API account's api_id, created_ts, expiration_ts, revocation_ts, and last_used_ts, each read as text, else null; every other field is dropped, and the records are exported keyed by the account's user_id read as text (empty text when it has none) through the export's credential pass. Decisions read created_ts, expiration_ts, and revocation_ts. When no record was requested the dataset is a marker naming the reason. | `api_id`, `created_ts`, `expiration_ts`, `revocation_ts`, `last_used_ts` | [Official documentation](https://docs.veracode.com/r/c_identity_intro) |
| `self_api_credentials` | HTTP | `GET /api/authn/v2/api_credentials` | Veracode Identity REST API | N/A | read | Only veracode_check_access requests it, to report whether it is readable; the record is neither exported nor read for a decision. | None | [Official documentation](https://docs.veracode.com/r/c_identity_intro) |

### Request construction

| Surface | Input | Exact value or rule | Required |
|---|---|---|---|
| `self` | client | Use the resolved API base URL: base_url, else VERACODE_API_BASE_URL, else the region's host, with any query, fragment, and trailing slashes removed. The region is the region argument, else VERACODE_REGION, else us, trimmed and lowercased: us or commercial is https://api.veracode.com, eu, europe, or european is https://api.veracode.eu, and us-fed, fedramp, or federal is https://api.veracode.us. | yes |
| `self` | headers | Accept: application/json; Authorization: VERACODE-HMAC-SHA-256 id={api_key_id},ts={timestamp},nonce={nonce},sig={signature} | yes |
| `self` | response | A JSON object carrying at least one of user_id, user_name, email_address, or roles; any other 2xx body, an empty one included, is a refusal, as is a status outside 200 through 299 after retries. | yes |
| `applications` | client | Use the resolved API base URL: base_url, else VERACODE_API_BASE_URL, else the region's host, with any query, fragment, and trailing slashes removed. The region is the region argument, else VERACODE_REGION, else us, trimmed and lowercased: us or commercial is https://api.veracode.com, eu, europe, or european is https://api.veracode.eu, and us-fed, fedramp, or federal is https://api.veracode.us. | yes |
| `applications` | headers | Accept: application/json; Authorization: VERACODE-HMAC-SHA-256 id={api_key_id},ts={timestamp},nonce={nonce},sig={signature} | yes |
| `applications` | response | A JSON object whose _embedded.applications array holds one application profile per entry, an entry that is not a JSON object being dropped (an _embedded that is itself an array is read in place of that array), and whose page object reports total_pages (else totalPages) and total_elements (else totalElements), each read as a number when present. An empty 2xx body, or JSON that is not an object, is read as a page holding no records; a status outside 200 through 299 after retries, or a 2xx body that is not JSON, is a refusal. | yes |
| `applications` | query:page | The zero-based page index: 0 for the first page, then one more than the previous page's | yes |
| `applications` | query:size | 100 | yes |
| `policies` | client | Use the resolved API base URL: base_url, else VERACODE_API_BASE_URL, else the region's host, with any query, fragment, and trailing slashes removed. The region is the region argument, else VERACODE_REGION, else us, trimmed and lowercased: us or commercial is https://api.veracode.com, eu, europe, or european is https://api.veracode.eu, and us-fed, fedramp, or federal is https://api.veracode.us. | yes |
| `policies` | headers | Accept: application/json; Authorization: VERACODE-HMAC-SHA-256 id={api_key_id},ts={timestamp},nonce={nonce},sig={signature} | yes |
| `policies` | response | A JSON object whose _embedded.policy_versions array holds one policy per entry, an entry that is not a JSON object being dropped (an _embedded that is itself an array is read in place of that array), and whose page object reports total_pages (else totalPages) and total_elements (else totalElements), each read as a number when present. An empty 2xx body, or JSON that is not an object, is read as a page holding no records; a status outside 200 through 299 after retries, or a 2xx body that is not JSON, is a refusal. | yes |
| `policies` | query:page | The zero-based page index: 0 for the first page, then one more than the previous page's | yes |
| `policies` | query:size | 100 | yes |
| `sandboxes` | client | Use the resolved API base URL: base_url, else VERACODE_API_BASE_URL, else the region's host, with any query, fragment, and trailing slashes removed. | `sandboxes` | headers | Accept: application/json; Authorization: VERACODE-HMAC-SHA-256 id={api_key_id},ts={timestamp},nonce={nonce},sig={signature} | yes |

|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| `applications`, `policies`, `dynamic_analyses`, `sca_workspaces`, `users`, `roles` | `page`, `page.total_pages`, `page.total_elements` | 100 | none | 50 | Pages of 100 records (size), indexed from 0 (page), up to 50 pages; the veracode_check_access probe reads only page 0. A page's total_pages and total_elements replace the values read before when present, and no _links.next link is read. An entry that is not a JSON object is dropped without marking the list partial. The list is partial when the walk reached its 50-page cap before a stop condition, met a non-empty page repeating the previous page's records, or ended with fewer records kept than the last reported total_elements. | Once a page has reported total_pages, the page whose index plus one reaches the last reported total_pages; Before any page has reported total_pages, a page holding fewer than 100 records, entries that are not JSON objects not counting; A non-empty page whose records' keys, in order, equal the previous page's, a record's key being the first of its guid, id, user_id, team_id, issue_id, and scan_id that is present and not null, else the whole record; the repeated page is not kept, and the list is partial; 50 pages read without another stop, which leaves the list partial unless the last reported total_pages was reached; A refused page, which makes the whole list a refusal |

## Rate limits

| Scope | Documented limit | Retry headers | Retryable statuses | Policy |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| Veracode Security Inspector | Veracode documents 500 REST API calls per minute from one IP address, answering a 429 with a retry-after time in seconds beyond it; the collector honors the retry-after time but does not track the limit. | `Retry-After` | 429, 500-599 | Retry 429, every status of 500 or above, network errors, and requests that time out up to 3 times after the first attempt. A Retry-After header in seconds or as an HTTP date measured against the client clock is honored, and a date already past asks for no wait; a wait longer than 30 seconds is cut to 30 seconds rather than refused, so a retry sent early that is throttled again spends an attempt. Without a readable Retry-After, and after a timeout or a network error, wait 500, 1000, then 2000 milliseconds. A request times out after timeout_seconds, else VERACODE_TIMEOUT, else 30 seconds, truncated and clamped to 1 through 300, and the timer covers reading the body. When the retries run out, the request fails with its path (never its query), the HTTP status, the timeout ("timed out after <ms>ms"), or the network error, and the number of attempts, and the dataset it was reading is reported as not collected. A 2xx body that is not JSON is a refusal and is not retried. |

### Primitive decision inputs

Every primitive is read from the named vendor surface or collector state before evidence lists are rendered or capped. Null and missing retain unavailable semantics; they are not empty inventories, false values, or zero counts.

| Finding | Input | Portable definition |
|---|---|---|
| `VERACODE-01` | `applications_readable` | Semantic owner: `VERACODE-01.applications_readable`. Type/domain: boolean. Compared literal domain: true. Source/owner: Veracode collector projection from `GET /appsec/v1/applications` (applications). Completeness/sample semantics: The primitive is calculated from the uncapped collector state before any 100-item finding preview or export sample; list cardinalities therefore refer to every item the collector obtained. Null/missing meaning: the named source did not establish this primitive; null or absence cannot independently satisfy a passing rule. It feeds executable derived facts `veracode_01_branch_01_matches`. Portable meaning: True when the GET /appsec/v1/applications walk returned without a refusal. |
| `VERACODE-01` | `application_count` | Semantic owner: `VERACODE-01.application_count`. Type/domain: number. Compared literal domain: 0. Source/owner: Veracode collector projection from `GET /appsec/v1/applications` (applications). Completeness/sample semantics: The primitive is calculated from the uncapped collector state before any 100-item finding preview or export sample; list cardinalities therefore refer to every item the collector obtained. Null/missing meaning: the named source did not establish this primitive; null or absence cannot independently satisfy a passing rule. It feeds executable derived facts `veracode_01_branch_02_matches`. Portable meaning: Number of application profiles the walk kept, entries that are not JSON objects being dropped. |
| `VERACODE-01` | `stale_static_scan_application_count` | Semantic owner: `VERACODE-01.stale_static_scan_application_count`. Type/domain: number. Compared literal domain: 0. Source/owner: Veracode collector projection from `GET /appsec/v1/applications` (applications). Completeness/sample semantics: The primitive is calculated from the uncapped collector state before any 100-item finding preview or export sample; list cardinalities therefore refer to every item the collector obtained. Null/missing meaning: the named source did not establish this primitive; null or absence cannot independently satisfy a passing rule. It feeds executable derived facts `veracode_01_branch_03_matches`. Portable meaning: Number of applications whose published date for STATIC is more than N whole days before the assessment time, N being the max_scan_age_days argument read as a number (a finite number, or a string that is not blank and parses as one), else 90, truncated to a whole number and clamped to 1 through 3650. An application's scans of a type are the JSON object entries of its scans array whose scan_type, read as text and uppercased, is that type. A scan is published when its status, read as text and uppercased, is PUBLISHED, PUBLISHED_TO_VENDOR, PUBLISHED_TO_ENTERPRISE, or PUBLISHED_TO_ENTERPRISEINT, and an application's published date for a type is the latest modified_date that parses as a date among its published scans of that type. A value reads as text when it is a string that is not blank, trimmed, or a finite number, as ECMAScript writes it. A value parses as a date when it reads as text and ECMAScript Date.parse reads that text. Whole days before the assessment time are the milliseconds from a date to the assessment time divided by 86400000 and rounded down. |
| `VERACODE-01` | `no_static_scan_application_count` | Semantic owner: `VERACODE-01.no_static_scan_application_count`. Type/domain: number. Compared literal domain: 0. Source/owner: Veracode collector projection from `GET /appsec/v1/applications` (applications). Completeness/sample semantics: The primitive is calculated from the uncapped collector state before any 100-item finding preview or export sample; list cardinalities therefore refer to every item the collector obtained. Null/missing meaning: the named source did not establish this primitive; null or absence cannot independently satisfy a passing rule. It feeds executable derived facts `veracode_01_branch_03_matches`. Portable meaning: Number of applications without a scan of type STATIC. An application's scans of a type are the JSON object entries of its scans array whose scan_type, read as text and uppercased, is that type. A scan is published when its status, read as text and uppercased, is PUBLISHED, PUBLISHED_TO_VENDOR, PUBLISHED_TO_ENTERPRISE, or PUBLISHED_TO_ENTERPRISEINT, and an application's published date for a type is the latest modified_date that parses as a date among its published scans of that type. A value reads as text when it is a string that is not blank, trimmed, or a finite number, as ECMAScript writes it. A value parses as a date when it reads as text and ECMAScript Date.parse reads that text. |
| `VERACODE-01` | `unpublished_static_scan_application_count` | Semantic owner: `VERACODE-01.unpublished_static_scan_application_count`. Type/domain: number. Compared literal domain: 0. Source/owner: Veracode collector projection from `GET /appsec/v1/applications` (applications). Completeness/sample semantics: The primitive is calculated from the uncapped collector state before any 100-item finding preview or export sample; list cardinalities therefore refer to every item the collector obtained. Null/missing meaning: the named source did not establish this primitive; null or absence cannot independently satisfy a passing rule. It feeds executable derived facts `veracode_01_branch_04_matches`. Portable meaning: Number of applications with scans of type STATIC but none published. An application's scans of a type are the JSON object entries of its scans array whose scan_type, read as text and uppercased, is that type. A scan is published when its status, read as text and uppercased, is PUBLISHED, PUBLISHED_TO_VENDOR, PUBLISHED_TO_ENTERPRISE, or PUBLISHED_TO_ENTERPRISEINT, and an application's published date for a type is the latest modified_date that parses as a date among its published scans of that type. A value reads as text when it is a string that is not blank, trimmed, or a finite number, as ECMAScript writes it. A value parses as a date when it reads as text and ECMAScript Date.parse reads that text. |
| `VERACODE-01` | `undated_static_scan_application_count` | Semantic owner: `VERACODE-01.undated_static_scan_application_count`. Type/domain: number. Compared literal domain: 0. | `VERACODE-04` | `overdue_application_count` | Semantic owner: `VERACODE-04.overdue_application_count`. Type/domain: number. Compared literal domain: 0. Source/owner: Veracode collector projection from `GET /appsec/v1/applications` (applications), `GET /appsec/v1/policies` (policies). Completeness/sample semantics: The primitive is calculated from the uncapped collector state before any 100-item finding preview or export sample; list cardinalities therefore refer to every item the collector obtained. Null/missing meaning: the named source did not establish this primitive; null or absence cannot independently satisfy a passing rule. It feeds executable derived facts `veracode_04_branch_04_matches`. Portable meaning: Number of overdue applications. An application's assigned policies are the JSON object entries of its profile.policies, and an assigned policy resolves to the last returned policy whose guid, read as text, is the same, a guid that does not read as text counting as empty text. Each resolved policy adds one requirement per JSON object entry of its scan_frequency_rules whose frequency, read as text and uppercased, is ONCE (at least once) or WEEKLY (7 days), MONTHLY (31 days), QUARTERLY (92 days), SEMI_ANNUALLY (183 days), ANNUALLY (366 days), EVERY_18_MONTHS (548 days), EVERY_2_YEARS (731 days), or EVERY_3_YEARS (1096 days), for the scan type that the entry's scan_type gives, read as text and uppercased, else ANY. An application whose profile.business_criticality reads as text adds an ANY requirement of N days, N being the critical_scan_interval_days argument read as a number (a finite number, or a string that is not blank and parses as one), else 7, truncated to a whole number and clamped to 1 through 3650 when that text uppercases to VERY_HIGH, and otherwise the standard_scan_interval_days argument read as a number (a finite number, or a string that is not blank and parses as one), else 31, truncated to a whole number and clamped to 1 through 3650. Only the strictest requirement per scan type counts: the one with the fewest days, the first on ties, the policies' requirements coming in assignment and rule order before the business criticality one. A counted requirement of type ANY is unconfirmed when the application's last_completed_scan_date does not parse as a date, and overdue when that date is more whole days before the assessment time than the requirement's number of days. A counted requirement of another type is overdue when the application has no scan of that type, unconfirmed when it has none published or no published date for that type, and overdue when that published date is more whole days before the assessment time than the requirement's number of days; an at-least-once requirement is never overdue by age. An application is overdue when one of its counted requirements is overdue, and otherwise unconfirmed when one is unconfirmed or one of its assigned policies does not resolve. An application's scans of a type are the JSON object entries of its scans array whose scan_type, read as text and uppercased, is that type. A scan is published when its status, read as text and uppercased, is PUBLISHED, PUBLISHED_TO_VENDOR, PUBLISHED_TO_ENTERPRISE, or PUBLISHED_TO_ENTERPRISEINT, and an application's published date for a type is the latest modified_date that parses as a date among its published scans of that type. A value reads as text when it is a string that is not blank, trimmed, or a finite number, as ECMAScript writes it. A value parses as a date when it reads as text and ECMAScript Date.parse reads that text. Whole days before the assessment time are the milliseconds from a date to the assessment time divided by 86400000 and rounded down. |

Each sampled analysis's scan list is read from GET /was/configservice/v1/analyses/{analysis_id}/scans, and the first 10 returned scans of each are inspected by reading GET /was/configservice/v1/scans/{scan_id}/configuration. |

When the GET /srcclr/v3/workspaces walk returned without a refusal and kept a workspace, each sampled application without upload-and-scan SCA has its linked project list read from GET /srcclr/v3/applications/{application_guid}/projects, its linked projects being the JSON object entries of the response's linked_projects array; otherwise no linked project list is requested. |

The sampled API accounts are the first 200 active API accounts in user list order, and each has its credential record read from GET /api/authn/v2/api_credentials/user_id/{user_id}. A user is active when its active is the boolean true or a string that trims to true in any letter case, and an API account when its account_type, read as text and uppercased, is API or its api_user is the boolean true or a string that trims to true in any letter case. An active human is an active user that is not an API account and whose login_enabled is the boolean true or a string that trims to true in any letter case. A value reads as text when it is a string that is not blank, trimmed, or a finite number, as ECMAScript writes it. |

### Ordered decision rules

Rules are evaluated from lowest order number to highest. The first matching condition determines the finding status; later rules are not evaluated.

A `matches` condition performs a regular-expression search; anchors are required for whole-value matching, and an `i` flag requests case-insensitive matching. A `ratio` condition divides the numerator by the denominator, applies the declared scale, and rounds to the declared decimal places by choosing the nearest value with exact half cases rounded toward positive infinity; a zero, null, or missing denominator does not match.

### Executable criterion examples

Each example's facts are evaluated against the ordered rules above when the spec is built; the build fails if the first matching rule does not produce the expected status.

| Finding | Case | Facts | Expected | Reason |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `VERACODE-01` | compliant | `applications_readable` = true; `application_count` = 12; `applications_complete` = true; `stale_static_scan_application_count` = 0; `no_static_scan_application_count` = 0; `unpublished_static_scan_application_count` = 0; `undated_static_scan_application_count` = 0 | pass | Every application's newest published static scan is within max_scan_age_days. |
| `VERACODE-01` | noncompliant | `applications_readable` = true; `application_count` = 12; `applications_complete` = true; `stale_static_scan_application_count` = 2; `no_static_scan_application_count` = 0; `unpublished_static_scan_application_count` = 0; `undated_static_scan_application_count` = 0 | fail | Two applications' newest published static scans are older than max_scan_age_days. |
| `VERACODE-01` | noncompliant | `applications_readable` = true; `application_count` = 12; `applications_complete` = true; `stale_static_scan_application_count` = 0; `no_static_scan_application_count` = 1; `unpublished_static_scan_application_count` = 0; `undated_static_scan_application_count` = 0 | fail | One application has only dynamic and SCA scans. |
| `VERACODE-01` | partial | `applications_readable` = true; `application_count` = 12; `applications_complete` = true; `stale_static_scan_application_count` = 0; `no_static_scan_application_count` = 0; `unpublished_static_scan_application_count` = 1; `undated_static_scan_application_count` = 0 | warn | One application's only static scan is still in progress. |
| `VERACODE-01` | partial | `applications_readable` = true; `application_count` = 12; `applications_complete` = true; `stale_static_scan_application_count` = 0; `no_static_scan_application_count` = 0; `unpublished_static_scan_application_count` = 0; `undated_static_scan_application_count` = 1 | warn | One application's published static scan carries no modified_date. |
| `VERACODE-01` | partial | `applications_readable` = true; `application_count` = 12; `applications_complete` = false; `stale_static_scan_application_count` = 0; `no_static_scan_application_count` = 0; `unpublished_static_scan_application_count` = 0; `undated_static_scan_application_count` = 0 | warn | The application walk stopped at its page cap. |
| `VERACODE-01` | partial | `applications_readable` = true; `application_count` = 0 | manual | The application inventory came back empty, which is not a compliant inventory. |
| `VERACODE-01` | unreadable | `applications_readable` = false | manual | The application inventory was refused. |
| `VERACODE-04` | compliant | `applications_readable` = true; `application_count` = 12; `applications_complete` = true; `policies_readable` = true; `policies_complete` = true; `overdue_application_count` = 0; `unconfirmed_frequency_application_count` = 0; `no_frequency_requirement_application_count` = 0 | pass | Every application meets its strictest scan frequency requirement. |

## Collection states

| State | Required rendering |
|---|---|
| complete | complete: proven API exhaustion or a successful single-object read. |
| truncated | truncated: preserve seen and total when available plus the exact stop reason. |
| unreadable | unreadable: render data and counts as null and retain a scrubbed error envelope. |
| denied | denied: render null evidence with the endpoint and HTTP status, never an empty inventory. |
| not requested | not_requested: identify the unreadable parent dependency and do not invent an HTTP status. |
| not configured | not_configured: identify the absent optional feature or credential without treating it as compliant. |

## Integration-specific scrubbing

Shared contract version: 1.1.

Projection stage: Responses are kept as read, except that each sampled analysis's scans are cut to scan_id and a scrubbed target_url, and each scan configuration to a summary that keeps the authentication method names but none of their contents. Every core_data and analysis JSON file is written through the credential pass: the value of each credential-named key, and the value member of an object whose name (else key) member is credential-named, becomes [REDACTED]; a string under a webhook-named key keeps only its URL's origin; a JWT-shaped string becomes [REDACTED]; and every other string loses URL userinfo, query, and fragment and has credential-shaped text scrubbed. metadata.json, analysis/summary.md, the compliance reports, and QUICK_REFERENCE.md are written as generated.

Sensitive fields and values: api_key_id, api_key_secret, veracode_api_key_id, veracode_api_key_secret, Authorization, authentications, crawl_script_data

Credential formats: Veracode API key ID and hex API key secret, used to sign every request with VERACODE-HMAC-SHA-256

Reviewed benign exceptions: Stable non-secret resource identifiers and public documentation URLs remain visible unless carried in a credential field.

Integration-specific rules:

- A refused response is reported with its status line and, when its body is JSON, the text of its message, error_description, error, and each _embedded.errors[] detail (else message) joined by semicolons, whitespace runs collapsed, cut to 240 characters, and scrubbed of the key ID and secret; a body that is not JSON is described by its media type and byte length and never echoed.
- A 2xx response whose body is not JSON is described by its media type and byte length and never echoed.
- A dataset that was not requested because the inventory it depends on was refused is exported as an explicit marker naming the reason, never as an empty dataset.

Projected fields by surface:

| Surface | Allowed fields |
|---|---|
| `self` | `user_id`, `user_name`, `email_address`, `roles.role_name` |
| `applications` | `guid`, `profile.name`, `profile.policies.guid`, `profile.policies.policy_compliance_status`, `profile.business_criticality`, `profile.business_unit.name`, `profile.teams`, `profile.upload_and_scan_sca_enabled`, `scans.scan_type`, `scans.status`, `scans.modified_date`, `last_completed_scan_date` |
| `policies` | `guid`, `name`, `type`, `finding_rules`, `sev0_grace_period`, `sev1_grace_period`, `sev2_grace_period`, `sev3_grace_period`, `sev4_grace_period`, `sev5_grace_period`, `scan_frequency_rules.scan_type`, `scan_frequency_rules.frequency` |
| `sandboxes` | None |
| `dynamic_analyses` | `analysis_id`, `name` |
| `dynamic_analysis_scans` | `scan_id`, `target_url` |
| `dynamic_scan_configurations` | `auth_configuration.authentications`, `crawl_configuration.disabled`, `crawl_configuration.crawl_script_data`, `target_url`, `target_url.url`, `scan_setting.allowed_hosts`, `allowed_hosts` |
| `findings` | `issue_id`, `finding_details.severity`, `finding_status.status`, `finding_status.resolution`, `finding_status.resolution_status`, `finding_status.first_found_date`, `annotations.action`, `annotations.comment` |
| `summary_reports` | `static_analysis.modules.module.loc`, `static_analysis.modules.module.numflawssev5`, `static_analysis.modules.module.numflawssev4` |
| `sca_workspaces` | `id`, `name` |
| `sca_vulnerability_issues` | `severity`, `vulnerability.cvss3_score`, `vulnerability.cvss2_score`, `vulnerability.cve`, `library.name` |
| `sca_license_issues` | `license.risk`, `license.name`, `library.name` |
| `sca_libraries` | None |
| `sca_application_projects` | `linked_projects.name`, `linked_projects.id`, `linked_projects.workspace.name`, `linked_projects.last_scan_date` |
| `users` | `user_id`, `user_name`, `email_address`, `active`, `login_enabled`, `account_type`, `api_user`, `roles.role_name`, `teams`, `no_teams_required`, `last_login`, `saml_user` |
| `teams` | None |
| `roles` | `role_name`, `ignore_team_restrictions` |
| `api_credentials` | `api_id`, `created_ts`, `expiration_ts`, `revocation_ts`, `last_used_ts` |
| `self_api_credentials` | None |

| `compliance/stig.md` | markdown | Always. | The runtime-generated human-readable compliance report. | UTF-8 text. |
| `compliance/irap.md` | markdown | Always. | The runtime-generated human-readable compliance report. | UTF-8 text. |
| `compliance/ismap.md` | markdown | Always. | The runtime-generated human-readable compliance report. | UTF-8 text. |
| `_errors.log` | text | Only when some reads fail but the bundle still completes. | The runtime-generated bundle metadata or operator guidance. | UTF-8 text. |

### Record schemas

#### finding

- `id`
- `title`
- `severity`
- `status`
- `summary`
- `evidence`
- `framework mappings`

#### collection_marker

- `collected`
- `status`
- `endpoint`
- `error`

#### bundle_result

- `outputDir`
- `zipPath`
- `fileCount`
- `findingCount`
- `errorCount`

#### assessment

- `title or category`
- `summary`
- `findings`
- `errors when collection was partial`

#### pagination_state

- `items`
- `pagesFetched`
- `totalPages`
- `totalElements`
- `complete`
- `notes`

JSON formatting: UTF-8 JSON with two-space indentation and a trailing newline.

Overwrite policy: Allocate the resolved API base URL's host (with its port when it has one), lowercased with every run of characters outside a-z, 0-9, dot, underscore, and hyphen replaced by one hyphen, trimmed of leading and trailing hyphens, and cut to 80 characters (veracode when nothing is left), followed by -audit-bundle, or by that and the first of -2 through -50 whose directory and zip are both free, and fail rather than overwrite.

Path safety: Resolve beneath the configured output root and reject traversal, unsafe parents, files, and symbolic-link escapes.

Archive pairing: Write a sibling zip named from the allocated bundle directory's name plus .zip in the output root.
